Bitcoin Faces First Back-to-Back Annual Losses in History, But There's a Catch

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Bitcoin Faces First Back-to-Back Annual Losses in History, But There's a Catch

Bitcoin is flirting with a historical first that bulls probably don’t want to hear: Two consecutive years of losses, something the cryptocurrency has never experienced in its 18-year history.

The world's largest cryptocurrency finished 2025 down 6.34% and has lost another 15.65% so far in 2026, trading around $73,850 on November 10. If Bitcoin fails to recover above its annual opening price of roughly $87,500 before December 31, it will close two calendar years in the red for the first time.

Historical annual returns show Bitcoin has experienced several brutal crashes since its creation, but each negative calendar year has been followed by a positive one. Even the disastrous 2018 and 2022 bear markets failed to produce consecutive annual losses.

The historical pattern is clear. Its significance, however, deserves a closer look.

Bitcoin's previous annual crashes were considerably more painful than its current losing streak. The cryptocurrency plunged 73.56% in 2018 and another 64.27% in 2022, wiping out most of investors' holdings in each respective year.

By comparison, Bitcoin's 6.34% decline in 2025 was relatively mild. Add the roughly 16% loss recorded this year, and the combined decline comes to approximately 21% after accounting for compounding.

That's a fraction of the losses investors endured in previous crypto winters, despite the market potentially recording a longer sequence of negative annual returns.

Bitcoin's performance in 2024 also provides some perspective. The cryptocurrency surged more than 120% that year, meaning investors who bought at the beginning of 2024 would still be sitting on substantial gains despite the subsequent decline.

The picture changes considerably when measuring losses from the market's actual peak instead of calendar-year boundaries.

Bitcoin reached approximately $126,000 in October 2025 before crashing below $58,000 during 2026. That represented a decline exceeding 50%, a significantly more painful move than the 21% combined loss reflected in the two annual candlesticks.

Analysts have pointed to Bitcoin's increasingly institutional investor base, spot exchange-traded funds, and deeper market liquidity as potential explanations for its comparatively smaller bear-market drawdowns.

Those structural changes may help explain why Bitcoin's current market cycle looks different from previous ones, although they have not eliminated major price crashes.

There is also a distinction between the length of a losing streak and the severity of the losses. A market can decline slowly over several calendar years without inflicting the same damage as a single catastrophic year.

And Bitcoin has already demonstrated that a bad year doesn't necessarily translate into a prolonged downturn. The cryptocurrency gained roughly 92% in 2019 following its 2018 collapse and surged more than 155% in 2023 after its disastrous 2022 performance.

For now, Bitcoin has 50 days left in 2026 to avoid its first back-to-back annual losses. A third consecutive decline in 2027 would extend the streak further, but that scenario is still hypothetical.

At its November 10 price of approximately $73,850, Bitcoin would need to gain about 18.5% to recover its 2026 opening price and finish the year in positive territory.

$7.6 Billion Takeover: easyJet Shareholders Back Apollo Deal That Will Take The Airline Private

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Shareholders at British budget airline easyJet have approved a deal worth £5.7 billion ($7.57 billion) for the private equity firm Apollo to take over the carrier. It will make the airline privately owned. Apollo Funds will hold up to 49.9% of the company, while rollover shareholders (choosing shares rather than cash), will hold between 45.1% and 49.9%. The EU Trust will hold up to 5%.

The airline says that the deal will be completed by the end of March, likely requiring regulatory approval. When it comes into effect, 26 years of easyJet's presence on the London stock market will screech to a sudden halt.

97.07% of shareholders voted in favor of the takeover. The price paid was 715p ($9.50) per share. Sir Stelios Haji-Ioannou founded the airline in 1995 and owned 15% of the airline with his family. It is understood that he backed the takeover.

It is not surprising that the airline was looking for a buyer given that it had been consulting on proposals to axe approximately 50 roles at head office from its 2,300-strong team. EasyJet had previously been in contact with Castlelake, a US investment firm, about a takeover.

The table below shows the current easyJet fleet, according to Planespotters.net. It represents some of the assets that will come into Apollo's control and is entirely made up of Airbus narrowbody jets. It has also previously flown Boeing 737 and 757, and British Aerospace BAe 146.

"EasyJet is pleased to announce that, at the Court Meeting and General Meeting held earlier today in connection with the Acquisition, the requisite majority of Scheme Shareholders voted to approve the Scheme at the Court Meeting and the requisite majority of easyJet Shareholders voted to pass the Resolution at the General Meeting."

The deal is being made with Eagle Bidco Ltd, a company that is indirectly owned by Apollo Funds and managed by affiliates of Apollo Capital Management, L.P. Apollo Funds is a US investment company. It is most active in the UK through its company, The Restaurant Group. This firm owns the Asian-style restaurant chain Wagamama.

Apollo began operations in 1990 and is currently headquartered in New York City. It ranked 29th in Private Equity International's 2024 ranking of the world's largest private equity firms.

In 2025, the company managed assets worth $840 billion with a net income of $3.5 billion. $99 billion of these assets were invested in private equity. These figures show that the easyJet purchase does not get close to breaking the bank for Apollo.

Will easyJet continue to exist as we know it today, or will its assets be sold off to the highest bidder?

Reuters has reported that Apollo will maintain the status quo once they have acquired easyJet. It will remain a standalone business, with the branding remaining consistent. There should also be comparatively little upheaval for the company's workers. The UK headquarters, along with the UK, Austrian and Swiss Air Operator Certificates will stay put. Apollo has also pledged to avoid redundancies in the first 12 months of its control over the airline.

Apollo has said it will help the airline by giving it access to additional capital. Becoming privately traded will also mean it can access longer-term planning. The essence of the strategy is to "do more" with what easyJet already has while ensuring it still offers customers a low-cost product, the Financial Times reports.

However, Apollo has some changes in mind. The first is a far more expansive loyalty program. The second is to introduce some premium elements on select routes.

Roku will stop selling smart home devices and refocus on streaming

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The company will stop selling video doorbells, its home monitoring system and other products.

Roku has confirmed it's getting out of the smart home market days after Fox completed its $22 billion takeover of the company. "We're sharpening our focus on Roku's core mission: being the top streaming platform," a Roku spokesperson told Lowpass. "As part of that, we plan to wind down sales of smart home hardware and will provide continued support and services for Roku Smart Home products."

Only two of Roku's smart home devices remain in stock on the brand's web store, the Roku Indoor Camera 360° and Video Doorbell & Chime. Products including smart light strips and bulbs, the wireless video doorbell, other cameras, smart plugs and Roku's home monitoring system are listed as out of stock. Lowpass noted that these devices are no longer available at many third-party retailers either. Amazon, for instance, now only offers refurbished models. The publication added that Roku has been gradually shifting away from its smart home products for the past several months — the company stopped mentioning such devices in its earnings reports earlier this year.

Roku started making a push into the smart home market in 2022 with rebadged versions of Wyze products. It started releasing its own smart home hardware last year.

Refocusing on streaming and associated hardware is a business decision, of course. Earlier this month, Roku posted its final earnings report before joining Fox. It revealed that, while revenue from devices grew by 33 percent year-over-year in the third quarter, its hardware losses more than doubled in that same timeframe, from $22.9 million to $48.6 million. It blamed those losses largely on increased costs of memory components.

The company noted that its device revenue is based on its own players and TVs, and not Roku-branded sets that other companies make. It added that its own TVs "exceeded expectations" last quarter. Since then, Roku has released its first OLED sets.

Spain sees widespread disruption in first general strike for 14 years

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Watch: Protesters gather across Spain for first general strike in 14 years

Transport and other public services across Spain have faced serious disruption in the first general strike for 14 years called by the main unions over housing and the high cost of living.

Demonstrations have taken place across the country, including in Madrid and Barcelona, where tens of thousands marched in the evening.

Spaniards are due to vote in parliamentary elections on 29 November.

The head of the big UGT labour union, Pepe Ãlvarez, said the strike was a success with "massive" participation, while Unai Sordo of the CC OO union said turnout was "very significant."

However, the Spanish Confederation of Business Organisations said participation was "low and uneven", depending on the areas and sectors involved.

The general strike took place little more than two weeks ahead of national elections in Spain

Some of the biggest disruption on Wednesday was seen in the main industrial hubs of Madrid and the north-eastern region of Catalonia where traffic was halted on several main roads.

The interior ministry said dozens of people were arrested for a variety of incidents and Spanish reports said estate agencies had seen attacks on their offices.

The strike was triggered by widespread protests over affordable housing and rising living costs, after the eviction of 87-year-old Maricarmen Abascal from a flat in Madrid where she had lived for seven decades. She died in hospital weeks later.

Rental prices in the capital have soared in recent years. The monthly cost of a 50m sq apartment in Madrid was an average of â¬1,170 in October, according to property portal Idealista, while Spain's average gross monthly salary is about â¬2,460 (Â£2,056).

That amounts to nearly half the average gross salary, well above the 30% level of affordability often recommended by housing experts and public institutions.

Tyres were set on fire in the city of Gijon in northern Spain

Socialist Prime Minister Pedro SÃ¡nchez called snap elections last month after parliament failed to approve his plan for housing reform.

Not since 2012, under the conservative government of Mariano Rajoy, has Spain held general strikes.

And while previous strikes have been problematic for the government in power, this one is different. SÃ¡nchez and his ministers have even encouraged workers to down tools and demand affordable housing.

"Your eight-hour day. Your pension. Your public healthcareâ¦ These were collective conquests," he wrote on social media, praising those who took part in previous strikes.

Dramatic eviction of woman aged 87 highlights Spain's housing shortage

Spanish PM SÃ¡nchez calls early election after housing protests

The conservative People's Party (PP), by contrast, has accused the government of attempting to weaponise the strike for electoral gain.

PP leader Alberto NÃºÃ±ez FeijÃ³o called the general strike "nonsense", calling the unions "mafia" and "trash". Feijoo's party is leading in the polls and party spokesman Miguel Tellado accused the government of exploiting the strike to warn voters against allowing a right-wing government to be formed, which looks the most likely outcome of the 29 November election.

SÃ¡nchez told Spanish TV this week: "What is nonsense is to question the rights of workers to strike."

Gonzalo Ãlvarez, who was taking part in the general strike, says 50% of his salary goes towards rent

Among those taking part in the general strike was Gonzalo Ãlvarez, 27, who was visiting different picket lines in central Madrid.

Although he works in sales, he has been volunteering for a housing union that tries to protect tenants and makes them aware of their rights.

The eviction of Maricarmen Abascal at the end of September "was the little fire" that sparked this protest, he told the BBC.

Ãlvarez knew the 87-year-old, who had faced a tripling of her rent under new landlords, and says he was deeply upset by her treatment.

He rents a small, one-bedroom flat with his girlfriend, a dental hygienist, in the city's southern Arganzuela disrict. The couple pay â¬1,000 between them, which means each spends about half of their salary on housing, even before other bills.

"It's absolutely crazyâ¦ with the food, with the electricity, with the water, with gas."

He sees no way of getting on the property ladder: "You have to collect an insane amount of money to be able to buy a house. So with these salaries, it's impossible."

With current rent and purchase prices, he believes buying somewhere is "completely impossible".

However, the vice-president of the CEOE, Spanish biggest business association, Miguel Garrido, criticised the strike, complaining that businesses had little to do with the price of housing.

"This is not a strike for labour-related reasons, but rather for political ones, and it is closely linked to the early calling of elections," he said.

Demonstrations were planned for some of Spain's biggest cities later in the day and the scale of support will be widely viewed as an indicator of public discontent ahead of the election.

Another reliable indicator of support for strikes is the level of demand for electricity with Spain's industrial sector.

Power usage in Spain fell by 7.5% during the early hours of Wednesday, according to data from operator Red ElÃ©ctrica. During the last general strike 14 years ago, equivalent consumption was down by more than 15%.

Jester Lands in Court: Florida Woman Accused of Stealing Properties With Forged Deeds

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A Florida woman has been accused of perpetrating a brazen deed fraud scam and illicitly transferred ownership of three properties in a scheme to fund her lavish lifestyle.

Melissa Jester, 41, of Bunnell, FL, is facing criminal charges after authorities say she forged a property owner's signature on deeds, transferred the land to a company she controlled, and pocketed the proceeds from one of the sales. She was arrested Nov. 6.

The victim, who owned three vacant lots in Florida for nearly two decades, recently discovered that the properties were transferred out of her name without her knowledge—and that one had been sold for nearly $30,000, according to the Flagler County Sheriff’s Office.

The victim contacted the authorities on Aug. 27 and said she had never authorized the sale or transfer of the properties. The three lots are located in the coastal city of Palm Coast, about 30 miles north of Daytona Beach.

An investigation was launched, and detectives uncovered what they described as a paper trail of forged documents, suspicious financial transactions, and a property sale that ultimately helped lead them to Jester.

Detectives traced a trail of forged documents and fraudulent financial transactions to Jester.

According to investigators, paperwork transferring ownership of all three lots to Florida Ohana Homes LLC, a company owned by Jester, was mailed to the Flagler County Clerk of Courts on June 29, 2026.

The paperwork appeared to have the property owner's signature on it. It was forged, investigators say.

Jester took further steps to hide her involvement by purchasing a money order on June 18 to pay the filing fees for the property under a different name, according to the affidavit.

The money order was purchased at the Amscot location on Palm Coast Parkway. The transaction did not require identification.

But the alleged scheme did not end there.

According to the Flagler County Sheriff’s Office, Jester, acting as managing member of Florida Ohana Homes LLC, then sold one of the stolen lots for $29,970 to an unsuspecting buyer on Sept. 10.

Surveillance video from the UPS Store on Belle Terre Parkway showed Jester signing the warranty deed and having it notarized. The money was transferred to the Bank of America account for Florida Ohana Homes LLC.

Authorities also allege that Jester transferred $8,000 from the business account into her personal account at VyStar Credit Union, where the money was spent on hotel stays and purchases throughout Flagler County.

By following the financial transactions, detectives were able to trace the money and recover a substantial portion of the proceeds from the alleged illicit sale.

More than $14,000 was seized from the company's Bank of America account and another $6,000 from Jester's personal credit union account. A total of $20,000 was recovered.

On Nov. 6, deputies located Jester driving in Bunnell, FL, and arrested her on charges of grand theft and obtaining property valued between $20,000 and $50,000 by swindle.

She is being held at the Sheriff Perry Hall Inmate Detention Facility on a $45,000 bond.

Flagler County Sheriff Rick Staly said the case stood out because, unlike many property fraud schemes involving suspects operating from distant locations, the alleged perpetrator lived nearby and conducted the transaction in person.

“Most of the deed fraud we see is run by scammers hiding in another state or another country. This one lived in Bunnell and signed the closing papers in front of a security camera in Palm Coast,” Sheriff Staly said. “Our detectives followed the trail of forgeries and money and recovered most of it for the victim.”

TV Ratings: ‘SNL’ Hits Two-Year High With Druski as Host

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Nov. 7 episode also had a big college football lead-in.

The Nov. 7 episode of Saturday Night Live scored its biggest same-day audience in two years with the hosting debut of comedian and online creator Druski. Having a big college football lead-in probably helped too.

SNL drew about 5.6 million viewers for the episode, which also featured musical guest the Strokes. It added about 440,000 viewers from the previous season high of 5.16 million a week earlier. The 5.6 million viewers is the best showing for SNL in almost exactly two years: The Nov. 2, 2024, edition delivered about 6.8 million viewers. Related Stories General News Trump Reflects on Republicans' Midterm Losses, Reacts to J.D. Vance's Own Analysis in '60 Minutes' Interview TV Elon Musk Is Portrayed on 'SNL': "The Worst Thing South Africa Has Ever Done"

The show also scored a two-year high in adults 18-49, with about 1.5 million viewers in that age range tuning in, and its best 18-34 rating in more than five years. NBC also says Saturday’s show was the most watched episode ever on Peacock, though it did not provide any numbers.

NBC also got big ratings in primetime Saturday with its telecast of Notre Dame’s 31-24 win over then No. 1 Miami in college football. The game averaged 9.1 million viewers on NBC and Peacock, making it the third most-watched primetime Notre Dame game since NBC and the university struck a broadcast deal in 1991. It was also easily the biggest game of NBC Sports’ primetime college football showcase (Big Ten or Notre Dame) so far this season.

The game led all college football telecasts for the weekend. The NBC-only portion of the game came in with about 8.6 million viewers, subject to adjustments as it started and finished outside the primetime window.

A couple other ratings notes from recent days:

• 60 Minutes averaged 7.77 million viewers for Sunday’s edition, which featured a post-midterms interview with President Donald Trump. While it was the most watched primetime show of the week outside of live sports, it was also at the lower end of Trump’s appearances on the show since he declared his first run for president in 2015. He has been on 60 Minutes eight times since then, and Sunday’s show drew the second smallest audience of the eight behind an April 2026 interview.

• Along with a strong election night, last week’s evening network newscasts also drew larger than usual audiences. All three posted their best viewer numbers since February, with ABC’s World News Tonight at 9.14 million viewers, the NBC Nightly News at 7.27 million and the CBS Evening News at 4.33 million (not including retitled telecasts used to game the Nielsen system).

MLB rumors: Yankees eye Alec Bohm, Tarik Skubal books more meetings, plus Nationals, Orioles, Mariners, more

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The uncertain 2026-27 Major League Baseball offseason is fully underway. The ongoing CBA negotiations and the near certainty of an owner-implemented lockout on Dec. 1 loom over all, and it may indeed mean a flurry of trades and free agent signings leading up to that date. That was certainly the case leading up to the last lockout of 2021-22. As for the present moment, the General Manager Meetings in Las Vegas are underway, and since there may not be any Winter Meetings this offseason (thanks to the lockout), the GMs' pow-wow could instead be a locus of activity.

Thanks to that prospect of a hastened pace and the ongoing GM Meetings, offseason rumors are flowing. Speaking of which, we'll be here each day to round up what's out there. You can find Wednesday's supply just below.

The New York Yankees are eyeing an upgrade at third base given the prolonged struggles of Ryan McMahon, and free agent Alec Bohm may be a potential target for GM Brian Cashman, according to the New York Post. Bohm, 30, is coming off a subpar walk year for the Philadelphia Phillies, but he has a career OPS+ of 101 overall. He also fared much better in the second half, and his season-long numbers were dragged down by a terrible April. Bohm is not much of a fielder these days, but the Yankees may be willing to live with that if they determine he can provide an offensive upgrade.

As for McMahon, Cashman already conceded to looking for a replacement.

"He's signed through next year for us, but I definitely know we need to improve production in a lot of different spots on the club if we can," he told reporters on Tuesday. "So we'll be open-minded to finding what we can run into if it's there."

The market for ace Tarik Skubal, far and away the top free agent available this offseason, continues to pick up steam, and it may be that he signs his next contract before the owners lock out the players on Dec. 1. The Los Angeles Dodgers, who traded for Skubal leading up to the deadline, the New York Mets, the Chicago Cubs, and the San Diego Padres are known to have varying levels of interest in Skubal. Now, the New York Post reports, the Toronto Blue Jays have entered the fray and emerged as Skubal suitors.

As for the Dodgers, they indeed plan to meet with Skubal, GM Brandon Gomes said. There could be a twist in their pursuit, though; The Athletic reports that the Dodgers are interested in Skubal on a short-term deal, presumably with a higher AAV like they gave to Kyle Tucker. But given the uncertainty of the CBA, would Skubal be willing to sign a contract that has him back on the market in just a few years?

Mets top executive David Stearns, meanwhile, emphasized on Tuesday that he and the club are willing to offer lengthy contracts to pitchers but that circumstances matter. That, in theory, should allay fears that he's not prepared to make a significant offer.

The San Diego Padres not long ago declined their club option on right-handed starter Nick Pivetta. However, they may not mean a parting of ways for club and player. Padres lead decision-maker AJ Preller on Tuesday told reporters, including Marty Caswell, that the team hopes to work out a new agreement with Pivetta so that he can return to San Diego for the 2027 season and perhaps beyond. Pivetta enjoyed a standout season in 2025, but his 2026, while excellent while on the mound, consisted of just eight regular-season starts because of elbow problems.

Dylan Crews, the 24-year-old outfielder for the Washington Nationals, has yet to reach the potential that made him the No. 2 overall draft pick in 2023. However, that ceiling is still there, and Crews is obviously young enough to realize his promise. Speaking of Crews, the Athletic reports that multiple teams have approached the Nats about Crews' availability in trade and that the Nats have not declared him off-limits. That of course doesn't mean a trade will happen, but a change of scenery for Crews may indeed be a winter possibility.

Outfielder Randy Arozarena was the Seattle Mariners' top player last season, and now he's a free agent. The M's, though, may not be interested in bringing back Arozarena and instead may be targeting fellow free agent outfielder Seiya Suzuki, the Seattle Times reports. Each veteran outfielder will be going into his age-32 season in 2027, and each produced at a high level in 2026.

After missing the playoffs in back-to-back seasons and trading away catcher Adley Rutschman leading up to the 2026 deadline, the Baltimore Orioles seem like a teardown candidate on paper. However, president of baseball operations Mike Elias batted down talk of a major roster makeover. "A lot of our key, core players are very unlikely to go anywhere," Elias told the media, including the Baltimore Banner, at the GM Meetings.

That presumably means that coveted shortstop Gunnar Henderson, among other notable names, won't be going anywhere this winter.

Janelle James Does Not Go to Trader Joe’s Anymore — She’s Famous

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The actress and stand-up talks about alarming fan interactions at the supermarket, tackling everything in Netflix special 'How Romantic' and what she thinks Ava from 'Abbott Elementary' has in common with Steve Urkel.

Janelle James has been performing comedy on stage for 15 years, but she’s not sure how much of her Abbott Elementary audience realizes that.

It’s one of the reasons why the stand-up and actress took her time in crafting her first hour-long special. How Romantic, a sprawling set that tackles politics, fame, and domestic violence, debuted on Netflix, Nov. 11. “I’m trying to introduce myself as a comedian to a whole new audience,” says James. “I’m trying to give a little bit about myself because I know people are curious about me because I’m pretty private.” Related Stories Business Netflix Launches New Brand Advertising Campaign In Engagement Push Movies Jann Arden, Cassandra Naud, Hunter Cardinal Lead 'Expiry Date' Dark Dramedy (Exclusive)

Speaking during a recent episode of The Hollywood Reporter podcast I’m Having an Episode (listen to the complete interview on episode on Spotify, Amazon Music, Apple), James discussed the long road to her special, what her Abbott Elementary character has in common with Steve Urkel and two reasons why you won’t be catching her at Trader Joe’s.

This is your first hour, and you chose to start with political material before moving onto many other topics. Was that strategic? How much do you think about your approach to discussing politics on stage?

Even in the beginning of my comedy career, I had to make a decision early on about what type of comedy I’d be doing. And when I started, that was the all the rage. Everyone was doing political comedy. I to make the decision of, “No, I won’t focus on that.” But, just like I did in the special, I’m gonna at least touch on it. Because I’m living in the world. But I never wanted to be stuck doing that because then people want you to do that forever. And that’s for everything. I never want to be put in a box for any reason because. I have a wide array of interests and opinions, you know. So, yeah, it is strategic how I approach it in this special. A lot of people haven’t seen me do stand-up before. I want to give them a sense of my sensibilities and who I am prime them for what type of performance I do. I’m pretty laid back.

You do appear very comfortable on stage. Was it always like that?

I always say my confidence is earned. I don’t have false confidence. I’ve been doing this for 15 years. And so this special where, I’m trying to do a lot. I’m trying to introduce myself as a comedian to a whole new audience. I’m trying to show people the breath of topics that I’m into. I’m trying to give a little bit about myself because I know people are curious about me because I’m pretty private. I’m also trying to tackle something that I am concerned about and has affected me, which is domestic violence. That’s what I end on. So I’m going a lot of places and the skill, after doing it for 15 years, is to be able to do that smoothly.

At one point you were based out of Champaign, Illinois, and you were going out into rural areas to perform. You performed in sundown towns. I’m assuming you saw some real some real interesting crowds. In hindsight, what’s the most dangerous situation you put yourself in?

All of them. (Laughs) Frequently, I was the only Black person for miles. I had people who’d come to those shows tell me stories about how the first Black person they saw was when they were 13 when they went to Chicago or something like that. They remember it like an event. Those are the places that I was performing. These places aren’t just like that racially, it was also very man-focused. Women would come to comedy shows as a bachelorette party or as a date with a man. But the groups were men who presumed they’d see other men perform. And then I would be here. I’m always a surprise, so that whole time was dangerous. But I was so into it and so obsessed, I think [training] that muscle with bombing, silence, being nervous, being dangerous, and grinding is what makes me look so comfortable now.

Did you always want to be a performer? I read that you lined up for MTV’s Wanna Be a VJ casting call when you were in high school.

I never wanted to be a performer. I did that with my friend. I just want experiences. Some people are very locked into what they do, you know? Like, “I’ve worked at this job for 30 years!” I always knew that wasn’t the path for me where, to lock into a gig or profession and that’s all I do. I am adaptable. Everything I do, I do it well, and it leads me to the next thing. I’ll do [Abbott Elementary] and who knows what I’ll do next. My joke on red carpets is that I’ll probably start a band. And I might not be joking. What would it be like to be a touring musician? I don’t know. I’m gonna find out. I think that upsets people who are fearful of change.

Abbott Elementary is in its sixth season, so it’s fair to assume the show doesn’t have that many years left. How much do you think about the realities of of what awaits you on the other side?

I think about being known as this character maybe for my whole life, which can be good and bad. The good is that I’ve done such a great job with this fictional character that people will never forget her. But there have been many sitcom characters, where you would say the actor’s name. Like, hey, that’s Jim Parsons. I am Ava, like an Urkel or a Screech.

Come on. We are not invoking Screech in this comparison.

Yes. But this is not in a bad way. It’s an iconic character. This character is in the lexicon forever. I think about that, but I don’t stress about it. Part of what I’m doing right now is showing people that Janelle also exists and that character comes from me. Hopefully that allows some separation. But I’m also in a different situation than most actors in that I have other skills. I don’t really stress about anything. One of the pluses of having started this later on in life is that I’m not a fucking idiot. I’ve spent my money wisely. I’ve already started planning for retirement. I take care of my mom. Starting later saved me from drinking alcohol and partying and sleeping with other comedians.

You say people think of you as Ava, but plenty of actors have more than one iconic character. Tracee Ellis Ross did it on Girlfriends and Black-ish. Julia Louis-Dreyfus had Seinfeld and Veep.

But people don’t go up to Julia Louis-Dreyfus and say “Elaine!” They say, “I love your work.” People come up to me as if I am Ava. That’s the difference. I know it sounds nuts.

Why do you think that is? Is it because of the mockumentary format?

I think so. One very creepy guy told me long ago in a Trader Joe’s: “When you look in the camera, it’s like you’re looking at me.” Sir, I am not. Could you please back up off me? The reason I bring up Urkel is because Jaleel White has talked about this, people being like, “You’re Urkel.” Unfortunately, his character is not as cool as mine.

I hope you stopped going to that Trader Joe’s.

I don’t go to any Trader Joe’s anymore. I’m famous.

Renowned Awards for the Newcomer: ID. Polo Wins Golden Steering Wheel, the Autonis Design Award and Is Voted German Car of the Year 2027

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The new ID. Polo is already garnering prestigious awards shortly after its market launch. They recognise both the overall package of the new all-electric small car and its design. Another prize was added yesterday evening: the ID. Polo received the Golden Steering Wheel 2026 in the Best car under 25,000 euros category. It had already been chosen as the overall winner of the German Car of the Year 2027 and won the coveted Autonis design award in the small car category.

With the Golden Steering Wheel award, the trade magazine AUTO BILD and the BILD am SONNTAG newspaper recognise outstanding new models every year. This year, the new ID. Polo impressed and received the Best car under 25,000 euros editorial award. In addition to its strong overall package, it was particularly highlighted that, with the ID. Polo, Volkswagen is bringing electric mobility into a price class that is affordable for many car drivers.

The award was presented to Thomas Schäfer, CEO of the Volkswagen brand, Head of the Brand Group Core and member of the Group Board of Management: “Three big awards within a few weeks, and now also the Golden Steering Wheel: the ID. Polo has made a dream start. Above all, however, customers, readers and experts confirm exactly what we wanted to achieve with this car, namely building true Volkswagen cars once more. Affordable, high-quality, suitable for everyday use and easy to operate. In fact: 100-per-cent Volkswagen. The fact that this has been recognised so quickly makes me proud of the entire team.”

Last week, the ID. Polo enjoyed great success at the German Car of the Year Award 2027: first, it won in the Budget car category for vehicles with a starting price of less than 25,000 euros. As a result, the small car qualified for the vote for the overall winner. After two intensive test days, the ID. Polo prevailed against four other strong class winners and won the coveted title of overall winner, making it the German Car of the Year 2027. An independent jury of 47 German and international motoring journalists decided on the award.

The design of the ID. Polo has also been well received: In the Autonis readers’ poll 2026 by auto motor und sport, the ID. Polo achieved first place in the small car category. Volkswagen achieved two category wins in the vote: in addition to the ID. Polo, the new T-Roc also won the Small SUV/Crossover category. The Volkswagen Multivan was a winner in the Vans category. For Volkswagen, the award as overall winner for the ID. Polo is also the first award for the new Pure Positive design language — with a clear-cut shape, a likeable appearance and surprising details.

ID. Polo also in the final of Car of the Year 2027. The new ID. Polo is also among the seven finalists in the renowned European Car of the Year 2027 award. For the first time in the history of the award, the list comprises exclusively of all-electric vehicles. After further test drives and the final evaluation by the international expert jury, the winner will be announced on 8 January 2027 at the Brussels Motor Show.

Electric. Practical. Affordable. The awards underline the positioning of the new ID. Polo as an all-electric compact Volkswagen for a broad audience. With a starting price of 24,995 euros (in Germany), a range of up to 454 kilometres and a luggage capacity of up to 1,240 litres (with the rear seat backrests folded down), it combines electric mobility with high everyday suitability, plenty of space and an attractive price-performance ratio.

Bitcoin Santa Rally Or Year-End Slump? What Needs To Happen For BTC To Break Out

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Bitcoin has fallen to its lowest level since August 20, and fear is back across the crypto market. Trader and analyst Tim Warren is not worried. He says the current pullback closely resembles early 2023, when Bitcoin dipped back to its 200-day moving average shortly before a new bull market began.

Bitcoin is trading near $74,000, right on its 200-day moving average. On the day of the latest inflation report, the price dropped on the hot reading, recovered all of its losses, and then fell again to land on that line.

Warren had expected this. In late September, he told his audience that sell signals were starting to pile up and that a correction back toward the 200-day average was likely. At the time, many traders were calling for a move above $90,000.

Warren believes Bitcoin’s bear market bottomed in the summer at $57,700. He pointed to several signs that the trend has turned:

The Crypto Fear and Greed Index has dropped back into extreme fear for the first time since August. According to Warren, Bitcoin is still about 17% higher than it was the last time sentiment was this low. To him, that points to “a screaming healthy correction” rather than the start of a new collapse.

Warren pointed to the ideas of Richard Wyckoff, a market trader from about a century ago, to explain the recent run of bad news. Wyckoff described big market players as one figure he called the “Composite Man.” In this view, large players push bullish headlines near market tops and frightening ones near bottoms.

The past few weeks have brought exactly that kind of news: the Binance hack, Democrats winning control of Congress and a hot inflation report. Warren had warned his community in September to expect “headlines that terrify you” during the correction.

Asked about a year-end rally, Warren said he mainly expects volatility. He had earlier predicted a new all-time high by January 20, but now says that is “probably not going to happen.”

He said big institutions are holding back until two issues are settled:

Warren believes the end of the Iran war could be the turning point. He noted that in recent inflation data, prices outside energy were running near or below the Fed’s 2% target. Energy costs were up about 10%, and transportation about 7% to 8%.

Once the war ends, Warren expects oil prices to fall sharply. That would bring inflation down faster than many expect, allow the Fed to stop raising rates and could even reopen talk of rate cuts, a setup that would favour Bitcoin.

Some of the biggest names in the XRP world now have the green light to sell shares in Evernorth Holdings, the Nasdaq-listed XRP treasury company trading as XRPN. The SEC declared Evernorth’s resale registration effective on November 9, covering up to 73.7 million shares. Arrington XRP Capital Fund holds the largest block at 33.82 million shares, nearly half the total. Ripple Labs follows with 18.14 million, SBI-linked entities hold 11.79 million and the Larsen Lam Children’s Remainder Trust has 7.15 million. Smaller stakes belong to a Pantera fund, GSR, gumi and Kraken parent Payward. Only about 19.1 million shares, roughly a quarter, are already issued. The rest would come from conversions, exchanges and warrant exercises. Registration does not mean any holder has sold, and some shares remain locked up. Evernorth will receive cash only from warrant exercises.

American Airlines brings fresh food back to NY-Chicago first class for first time since 2019

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

American Airlines said Wednesday it's bringing small, fresh "bites" to first class on flights between its hubs in New York and Chicago, the carrier's latest effort to win over higher-spending customers.

The changes start this month. Customers will be able to pick between two options, which include prosciutto and melon or apple cinnamon chia pudding for breakfast and a Thai noodle salad or mezze plate for lunch and dinner, American said Wednesday.

American last offered fresh food for first class on these routes in 2019, a spokeswoman said. Currently, customers in first class on these flights get a smaller, packaged snack. Airlines largely have limited food options for shorter flights, even in first class.

American has fallen behind Delta Air Lines and United Airlines in profits and investments in premium options. The airline has been working to step up those premium offerings in recent years with new cabins, lounges and plans for seatback screens on its narrow-body fleets.

His “Deep-Lens” analysis connects the dots between boardroom strategy and pitch-level recruitment, providing a level of transfer clarity that goes beyond surface-level rumours.

Everton are "reluctant" to revisit a move for Tottenham Hotspur striker Richarlison in January, sources have told Football Insider.

The Toffees made a move to reunite with their former star in the latter stages of the summer transfer window, but saw their attempt knocked back by the player.

Tottenham are now open to helping the Brazilian find a move away this winter, given that he has been frozen out by Roberto De Zerbi.

Sources say that Everton will only consider making another move for Richarlison if they fail to land their priority striker targets.

David Moyes' side are desperate to add another frontman to their arsenal, with Thierno Barry their only recognised striker at the moment.

Barry has already netted six goals from the opening 10 Premier League games, but the club are determined to get him more support at the top of the pitch.

Richarlison is firmly out of the picture at Tottenham, and there was a great deal of surprise when he turned down the chance to return to Everton in the summer.

However, with the player's stance now clear, there is a growing feeling at Everton that the ship has now sailed in terms of them making another move for him.

The Merseysiders are working hard to add another striker to their squad, but are not prepared to try and force a move for someone who doesn't want to be there.

Richarlison has also attracted significant interest from clubs in both Brazil and Turkey of late, with his future due to be settled this winter.

Everton have not taken him off the table as an option at this stage, but they would need to be unsuccessful in several other pursuits in order to turn back to him.

Football Insider revealed back in October that Everton are assessing a move for prolific Dinamo Zagreb striker Dion Beljo ahead of January.

The 24-year-old has plundered 13 goals from just 11 games in the Croatian top-flight this term, after netting 38 times in all competitions last season.

Despite Everton's interest, sources say that Dinamo are well-placed to negotiate a deal on their terms, with Beljo under contract until the summer of 2030.

The Croatia international has truly caught the eye in the last 12 months, and even scored in Dinamo's recent Europa League win over Sunderland.

Jamie Campbell Bower Is the Rising Style Star You Need to Know

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

When it comes to Hollywood’s red carpets, the menswear assortment is—more often than not—very safe. Snoozy, even. Sure, classic suits and tuxedos will always have a place at a splashy movie premiere, but whatever happened to fun? No one’s fun anymore!

That being said, there are a few actors who are taking a more eccentric and unexpected approach to their fashion choices. Take Alexander Skarsgard, and his freaky shoes. Or, more recently, there’s Jamie Campbell Bower, who has been bringing a unique fashion energy to his press tour for The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power this month. (The star recently joined season three of the hit Prime Video series.)

You may know Bower for his music, or for portraying the villainous Vecna on Stranger Things, but we are fast associating him with an impeccable sense of style. Throughout his press tour this month, he and his stylist Nathan Henry have been infusing traditional tailoring looks with cool, off-kilter accessories—like brooches and headpieces!—that make the outfits feel a whole lot more fresh and modern.

Last night in London, for one, Bower emerged wearing a black cape coat from Matières Fécales—the avant-garde Parisian label that is fast becoming a celebrity favorite. Punctuating his sleek outerwear? There was a bejeweled crown headpiece, oversized eyeball and flower brooches, and even a spider-like jewelry piece that fit like a glove. Talk about fun!

Wearing pieces by Wooyoungmi, John Richmond and Adelina Bernard.

Some of his other recent Lord of the Rings looks have been more subdued—but equally as slick and contemporary. For the New York premiere, there was a perfectly-slouchy black pinstriped suit with layered silver necklaces; For the Mexico City screening, he chose a black suit with a burgundy velvet lapel, and a crisp white shirt with a high collar and oversized lapels. These slight exaggerations of silhouette imbued his looks with a little personality: They’re not so formal or standard; rather, they have a little zest to them.

Given he is also a professional musical artist by day, Bower’s rock-and-roll sensibility could play a big part in why his red carpet style feels so noteworthy: He simply has that innate, effortless way of dressing that so many musicians seem to nail. (Think: Mick Jagger, Prince, Bowie.) And we need this energy from a whole lot more men in Hollywood. . . Life is short: Take a risk!

JetBlue unveils new first class seats to bring back profits

📅 2026-11-11  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

JetBlue Airways is unveiling its new domestic first-class seats Wednesday as more of the country's carriers bet on the premium travel boom to make them profitable again.

JetBlue announced it would offer a domestic first class — BlueFirst — in December 2024 and plans to start flying aircraft with the new seats and full on-board product next month. The carrier is showing off the new cabin at its home hub at John F. Kennedy International Airport on Wednesday. Retrofits will continue during 2027, CEO Joanna Geraghty told CNBC late last month.

The rollout comes as the New York airline tries to stabilize after last posting a profit in 2019. Last month, JetBlue executives sought to calm investors after shares in the airline hit a record low following a report that bondholders had contracted restructuring advisors.

"We have no intention, no plans to file for bankruptcy. We are not engaged with any restructuring company," Geraghty told CNBC in an interview on Oct. 27.

Geraghty told CNBC's Phil LeBeau on Wednesday that JetBlue has "met with a few of the creditors over the last couple of weeks as part of ... normal investor engagement." She said the company made it clear to bondholders that JetBlue doesn't need to reorganize.

The airline's shares are up more than 40% this month, more than other passenger carriers, as of midday Wednesday, though they are still off about 11% so far this year.

JetBlue is betting that resilient travel demand, especially for for roomier seats will help fuel its turnaround plan, which aims to get the carrier to an operating profit next year and $1 in adjusted earnings per share in 2028.

Airline executives have said they expect fares to stay elevated next year as they grapple with higher costs, including but not limited to fuel, such as labor and airport fees. Geraghty said Wednesday that even if fuel prices moderate next year, "I don't think fares are going to fall as much as people expect."

Airlines large and small are racing to outfit their cabins with roomier, more expensive seating options to help cover ever-higher costs, from jet fuel to labor to airport operation fees.

For example, a ticket in the new recliner first-class seats between Kennedy Airport and Fort Lauderdale-Hollywood International Airport in Florida is going for $847 roundtrip in the first week of March while standard economy is $446.

On Tuesday, budget carrier Frontier Airlines unveiled its new first class seats, which will take up the first two rows, and said it would outfit its Airbus fleet with premium seating that have extra legroom and in-seat power through row eight. The airline will take out a row of seating on its planes to make the pricier ones fit, chief marketing officer Bobby Schroeter told CNBC in an interview Monday.

JetBlue last month reported it ended the third quarter with $2.2 billion in liquidity and that its unencumbered asset base is now $7 billion, including $2.8 billion in aircraft and engines.

JetBlue had total debt of $8.4 billion as of Sept. 30, the carrier said in its earnings filing.

Geraghty told CNBC last month there is a "fundamental disconnect between the narrative in the bond market and what our financial results actually demonstrate."

When asked then whether JetBlue's eye on keeping costs down includes furloughs, layoffs or buyouts, Geraghty last month said "absolutely not."

West Brom told to ‘find the money’ and sign Blackburn Rovers star

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

EFL pundit Tom Garratt has urged West Brom to ‘find the money’ to sign Blackburn Rovers ace Andri Gudjohnsen during the January transfer window.

Speaking on the latest edition of the Pyramid Podcast, Garratt, hosting alongside Callum Fowler, believes Gudjohnsen could be the missing piece in the Albion jigsaw to help the Black Country outfit seal promotion back to the Premier League.

James Morrison’s men continue to be in the promotion conversation, heading into the latest international break unbeaten in their last six games and securing four victories in that period.

Their latest 3-0 romping of Millwall last weekend leaves Albion fifth in the second-tier standings, sitting just one point off the summit in such a compact top eight.

Much was made at the beginning of the season about West Brom’s lack of a clinical frontman, with it one of the main concerns as to why some connected with Albion feel a promotion push may fade away in time.

Controlling shareholder Shilen Patel may be able to fix the issue in the January transfer window, should funds become available, and it could well be the difference maker in whether they return to the big time or not.

So, with this in mind, well-known EFL pundit and West Brom supporter Tom Garratt has demanded the club make a deal to sign one of the best-performing strikers in the Championship, at this moment in time.

Despite having such an excellent summer transfer window, one criticism from some West Brom supporters at the club was not going all out to recruit a striker who could score regularly at Championship level.

Albion were reportedly interested in Southampton’s Cameron Archer, but missed out on a deal due to his high wages on the South Coast, with the 24-year-old eventually going on to move to French outfit Auxerre instead, where he has scored six goals in eight Ligue 1 games.

The Baggies were busy during the summer window revamping some of the forward line, replacing the outgoings of Josh Maja and Daryl Dike with Barney Stewart and Jimmy-Jay Morgan.

Morgan in particular has been a roaring success so far, grabbing eight goal contributions in 15 league matches so far for the club, but is not a conventional centre-forward, favouring drifting over to the left-hand side while a proper number nine stays central.

Stewart, since moving from Scottish side Falkirk, has found opportunities hard to come by and has netted just once for his new club so far, while Aune Heggebo in his second season at the club has also found himself in and out of the side, with most of his success coming off the bench and making an impact in recent times.

Thankfully, much of Albion’s early success has been down to players in other positions chipping in with goals, with the likes of Isaac Price notching six times and providing three assists, while fellow summer arrival Felix Horn Myhre now has three goals to his name in the blue and white stripes.

West Bromwich Albion signed Felix Horn Myhre for just £3.1 million in the summer ahead of Spanish outfit Elche

Despite that, Albion fans would be put at ease to see a clinical frontman lead the line from January onwards, with a regular source of goals badly needed for the Baggies to continue to fight towards the top end of the division.

Speaking on the latest episode of the Pyramid Podcast with Callum Fowler, EFL pundit Tom Garratt has demanded West Brom ‘find the money’ to sign Blackburn Rovers star Andri Gudjohnsen in January.

Gudjohnsen is already into double figures for goals from his opening 14 league games this season, notably scoring a brace at The Hawthorns against West Brom last month, while also netting in Rovers' 2-1 victory over West Ham last weekend.

And Garratt was gushing about the 24-year-old's strike against the Hammers, believing he is the second-best striker in the division and can help the Baggies turn their promotion hopes into a reality.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

Garratt said: “This is a plea to West Bromwich Albion again.

“Find the money, find the money somewhere, it’s there somewhere. (Tom) Fellows went for a lot of money. We have sold players in recent years, go and buy (Andri) Gudjohnsen.

“His movement for the goal is so, so good. Don’t get me wrong, it’s a ‘tap in’, but his movement to get there is unbelievable. He is the second-best striker in this league.”

There’s no doubt that what’s missing most in West Brom’s squad is the addition of a striker that can usually net between 20 and 25 goals in a full Championship season, with Albion continuing to bank on the likes of Isaac Price to perform and find the back of the net consistently.

But over time, that’s at risk of drying up, so Albion must do all they can at the turn of the new year to bring in a forward who has an eye for goal and can make a real difference at the top end of the pitch.

And while it’s likely to be one of the areas the Baggies hierarchy look to address, the chances of Blackburn’s Andri Gudjohnsen signing on the dotted line at The Hawthorns is bound to be extremely low.

West Brom fans, could you see the Baggies moving for Andri Gudjohnsen in January, or do you feel he's an unrealistic target? Drop your thoughts in the comments below!

After having much of a revamp during the summer transfer window, Albion are likely to not possess many financial funds in January, with clubs across the country much more active in the loan market as bigger sides to themselves decide to temporarily part ways with players who can’t break into their first-team crop.

Meanwhile, Gudjohnsen’s impact at Blackburn cannot be understated. His 10 goals so far have been a massive reason as to why Rovers sit on the periphery of the top eight, and while the Lancashire outfit have let go of some stars on the cheap in recent times, there’s no reason to suggest why Blackburn wouldn’t want a massive fee to part them from their Icelandic star - a price that only Premier League level sides can afford.

You do feel if Gudjohnsen was to move on from Ewood Park in January, then it would be permanently, so factoring in what would be such a high price to secure his services and an unwillingness to let him leave on loan, in particular to a Championship rival, you would think it’s a very low chance that West Brom will be Gudjohnsen’s next club.

However, there’s no reason why Albion shouldn’t be able to land a similar goalscoring forward on loan in January, with the winter window always throwing up such unexpected deals the closer the clock ticks towards the deadline.

Zoë Kravitz Is Back on the Red Carpet, Going for Gold, And Baring Her Abs

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

What does one wear to the premiere of a movie called How to Rob a Bank? If you’re Zoë Kravitz, it’s got to be something worth stealing. The actor made a glittering return to the red carpet in New York last night, attending the premiere of her new film alongside co-stars Nicholas Hoult and Pete Davidson. The actor opted for suitably decadent, gilded gold Saint Laurent ensemble, with plenty of skin on show—in one fell swoop, hitting two of her red carpet rules.

Kravitz wore a cropped, long-sleeved jacket in richly patterned gold brocade, with an embellished neckline and plush, dark brown faux-fur cuffs. Sitting low on her hips were matching straight-leg trousers, with intricate crystal embellishments and a sparkling floral buckle at the waistband. Barely-there metallic sandals, delicate earrings, and an undone updo with wispy tendrils framing her face finished things off. And there was a generous flash of those famously toned abs.

Kravitz has been all over New York of late, filming her forthcoming movie Die Alive alongside Jamie Dornan and Riley Keough while also enjoying some downtime with fiancé Harry Styles. Her off-duty uniform remains resolutely relaxed: The Row trousers, low-slung denim, little T-shirts, and well-loved outerwear. But when it comes to the red carpet, Kravitz has a rather different set of sartorial rules: Saint Laurent, lots of skin, and layers of gold.

At the CFDA Awards earlier this month, Kravitz joined fellow house muses Hailey Bieber, Rosé, and Anja Rubik in celebrating Anthony Vaccarello, who received the International Designer of the Year award. She wore a shimmering, liquid-gold, open-backed look from the spring 2027 collection, with a high neckline and a slinky, low-waisted skirt. Of course, Kravitz is no stranger to a little midriff exposure, either: Who could forget the ultra-low-slung, ab-baring recreation of a spring 1990 Yves Saint Laurent look, originally worn by Yasmeen Ghauri, that she wore for the Caught Stealing premiere last year?

Whether its molten metallics or the tiniest sliver of fabric, Zoë Kravitz knows exactly what works for her on the red carpet.

Michael Fassbender’s 2-Part Spy Thriller Officially in Jeopardy With No Renewal in 2026

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Spy thrillers have enjoyed a massive boom in recent years, with almost every major streaming platform committing significant investment to fuel the allure. Paramount+ has perhaps made the biggest bang for its buck thanks to two major hits in Taylor Sheridan's Lioness and The Agency, starring Michael Fassbender. The latter originally premiered in 2024 as an instant smash hit, earning a swift Season 2 renewal. That second chapter arrived in the summer, with improved quality, and after a cliffhanger ending, fans have been left wondering about its future.

The Agency Season 2 premiered with all 10 episodes released at once on Paramount+. The new season's critical and audience reception scores both saw significant bumps, from 66% to 92% and 79% to 93%, respectively. Though arriving amid stiff streaming competition, The Agency still managed to pull in 393 million minutes watched in its debut week, per Nielsen data, which ranked it the 9th most-watched show at the time. Paramount+, however, has yet to publish the season's official streaming numbers, which will offer better insight into its performance against other Paramount originals — a key metric that will affect its future.

Paramount+'s silence on The Agency Season 3 is rather concerning, especially considering that Season 2 was renewed mere weeks after the Season 1 premiere. The delay could, however, be due to Paramount and Warner Bros.' recent merger under Skydance. Given that such mergers often involve significant cost-cutting measures, there's no telling where this leaves The Agency. Also, offering more cause for concern is a recent update from series star Dominic West, who plays the CIA director. During an interview with The Playlist, he revealed that The Agency was facing a delay, saying, "I was talking to someone about it last night, and I think there was some delay about it for some reason."

Britain makes more detective television than any country has any business making, and somehow almost all of it is good. Here is the one you belong in, what it says about you, and the case for it being the best of the four.

You want the tea, the gossip and the vicar behaving oddly. Midsomer is a county where the scenery is beautiful, the fete is on Saturday and the murder rate is roughly that of a small war, and absolutely nobody involved thinks this is strange.

You feel other people's grief as if it were handed to you personally. Broadchurch is less a whodunnit than a study of what a small town does to itself when it finds out that the answer was always going to be somebody everybody already knew.

You go through things rather than around them. John Luther is the best detective in London and a liability to everybody who works with him, and the show is completely honest that those two facts are the same fact.

You say the thing and you do not dress it up. Vera Stanhope turns up in a hat and a mac, is rude to almost everybody, and is the only person in the room prepared to ask the question that the family has been waiting years for somebody to ask.

West, in the same interview, which primarily centered on his new HBO Max series, War, could not offer details as to the reason for the delay. He, however, expressed hopes that it was something the series could somehow navigate. "I don't know... But hopefully there will, yeah. I think we need to see more of the new head of the CIA," he said.

Should The Agency eventually earn a renewal, the likelihood of its return in 2027 now appears nearly impossible, given that Season 2 arrived about a year and a half after it was renewed. Even though Paramount+ announces the renewal before the year ends, 2028 would be a more plausible release year going by the established release timeline.

The Agency's first two seasons are streaming on Paramount+. Stay tuned for future updates.

Home Depot vs. Uber: One Stock Is Betting on a Housing Recovery, the Other on the Future of Transportation. Which Is the Better Buy for 2027?

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Strong earnings growth, attractive valuations, and an uncertain economic outlook are creating two very different opportunities for investors heading into 2027.

Home Depot (HD -0.69%) and Uber Technologies (UBER -1.57%) are two market leaders facing very different challenges heading into 2027. Home Depot is navigating a prolonged housing slowdown, while Uber continues to deliver strong growth despite a struggling stock price and growing uncertainty about the future of autonomous transportation.

For investors, the distinction between temporary economic headwinds and potential long-term disruption could be critical in determining which stock offers the better opportunity in the upcoming year.

Home Depot is a home improvement retailer that serves homeowners, contractors, and construction professionals. The company has increasingly focused on expanding its professional contractor business through acquisitions to strengthen its presence in roofing, building materials, and specialty construction products. This strategy helps diversify revenue beyond traditional do-it-yourself home improvement spending.

In fiscal 2025, Home Depot generated $164.7 billion in revenue, an increase of 3.2% year over year. However, net income declined 4.4% to $14.2 billion as elevated borrowing costs and sluggish housing activity weighed on demand. More recent results highlight continued challenges. In the second quarter of fiscal 2026, revenue increased 5.7% to $47.9 billion, but comparable sales grew just 1.7%, indicating relatively modest underlying demand despite contributions from acquisitions.

Home Depot remains a substantial cash-generating business, producing approximately $12.6 billion in free cash flow during fiscal 2025. However, its debt-to-equity ratio of roughly 5.1x reflects a leveraged balance sheet, while its current ratio stood near 1.1x. Although its cash generation provides financial stability, meaningful growth will likely depend on an improvement in housing activity and renewed spending on larger renovation projects.

Uber operates a global mobility and delivery platform connecting millions of consumers with drivers, couriers, and merchants. The company is positioning itself for the transition to autonomous transportation, focusing on integrating self-driving vehicles into its existing network rather than developing its own autonomous driving technology. Management expects more than 2,000 fully driverless vehicles operating on its platform by the end of 2027.

In fiscal 2025, Uber generated $52 billion in revenue, an 18% year-over-year increase. Net income reached approximately $10.1 billion, although a substantial portion reflected a one-time tax benefit. More recent results highlight continued operating momentum. In the third quarter of 2026, gross bookings increased 20% to $59.8 billion, while revenue climbed 10% to $14.8 billion. Adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) rose 31% to nearly $3 billion, demonstrating improving profitability as the platform expands.

Uber also maintains a strong financial position. In fiscal 2025, the company generated $9.8 billion in free cash flow, an increase of 42% year over year. At the end of September 2026, it held approximately $10.1 billion in unrestricted cash and short-term investments, providing substantial flexibility to invest in new technologies and expand its operations.

Home Depot's biggest challenge remains the housing market. Elevated mortgage rates and sluggish home sales have weighed on renovation spending, while competition from other home improvement retailers adds pressure on pricing and margins. Although lower interest rates could eventually revive demand, a prolonged housing slowdown could continue limiting growth.

Uber faces a potentially more significant long-term challenge from autonomous transportation. While the company is positioning its platform to accommodate self-driving vehicles, competitors could increasingly offer rides directly to consumers, threatening Uber's market share and pricing power. Regulatory uncertainty surrounding driver classification, along with competition from Lyft (LYFT +1.55%) and other mobility providers, introduces additional risks.

Uber appears to offer better value based on future earnings estimates, though Home Depot remains the more affordable option when comparing its price to trailing sales.

Valuation metrics include sourcing from Financial Modeling Prep (FMP) and may differ from other data providers.

Home Depot and Uber offer two very different investment opportunities heading into 2027. The distinction comes down to the nature of their challenges. Home Depot's struggles are largely cyclical, meaning a recovery in housing activity could revive demand for home improvement projects. However, with mortgage rates remaining elevated and remodeling spending expected to grow modestly, a meaningful rebound may take time.

Uber, meanwhile, is already delivering strong operating results. Its latest quarterly gross bookings increased 20%, while adjusted EBITDA climbed 31%. Despite that momentum, the stock has struggled as investors question how autonomous vehicles could reshape its business. That uncertainty is legitimate, particularly as self-driving operators explore offering rides directly to consumers. However, Uber's established customer network, growing delivery business, and substantial cash generation provide competitive advantages as the industry evolves.

For investors looking toward 2027, Uber is my preferred buy. Trading at 20.4 times forward earnings compared with Home Depot's 23.3 times, Uber offers a compelling combination of faster growth and a slightly lower earnings valuation. Autonomous transportation poses meaningful long-term risks, but I believe the company's current operating momentum and financial strength make a stronger investment case than waiting for an uncertain housing recovery.

‘Wake-Up Call’: Trump’s Former Housing Commissioner Says New Congress Must ‘Get Serious’ About Housing Affordability

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Democrats and Republicans both understand Americans are broadly worried about the cost of living, and a former Trump administration official says it's time for both parties to take action.

In a bitterly divided Washington, DC, it remains to be seen whether lawmakers will heed that call when the 120th Congress convenes in January, with Democrats back in control of both the House and Senate.

After campaigning hard on affordability concerns, Democratic leaders say they'll use their new majorities to focus on the cost of living. Republican leaders admit they didn't heed voter concerns about the economy. Meanwhile, President Donald Trump has threatened to use his veto power freely.

The summer's 21st Century Road to Housing Act, with its dozens of provisions aimed at cutting red tape, was a rare example of bipartisan consensus. What follows that landmark legislation remains an open question.

Republicans want more deregulation while Democrats aim for more support for housing. And each side must now contend with an upswell of hard-liners pushing policies that could radically change the market.

Many of the prominent names in both parties who built the housing bill are returning, including Rep. French Hill (R-AR), Rep. Maxine Waters (D-CA), Sen. Elizabeth Warren (D-MA), and Sen. Tim Scott (R-SC). They're likely to return to the House and Senate committees on housing and banking.

"Now is a wake-up call," Trump's former Federal Housing Commissioner Frank Cassidy tells Realtor.com®. "Senate, House, White House. Everyone needs to get serious about tackling and addressing housing affordability. It can't be ignored anymore."

Congress had some bipartisan momentum to provide tax incentives that encourage the buying and selling of homes.

Colin Allen, executive director of the American Property Owners Alliance and a former housing issues adviser to Sen. Lindsey Graham (R-SC), said that could mean some ideas pitched in this Congress could come back as part of larger bills.

"They have good working relationships and have already done some bipartisan work," Allen said.

Take, for instance, the Neighborhood Homes Investment Act, which would create a new tax credit to buy and renovate homes in economically distressed areas. The bill drew 70 sponsors from both parties.

Real estate groups put in significant groundwork this year to update the capital gains tax scheme for the first time in more than 30 years. The More Homes on the Market Act came close to 200 sponsors in Congress. It would double the exclusion for many filers. It's been floating around Congress for several years and has been a target of the National Association of Realtors® and other groups.

Trump expressed some support for changing the capital gains tax scheme, though a specific plan hasn't yet materialized from the White House.

"We've done a lot of legwork to get folks behind it," Allen said. "If we're looking at a larger bipartisan bill, you can see a scenario where a few of those ideas get together. The debt limit is coming up, and there are a few other opportunities to package together."

Hill and Republicans have lobbied for more changes to the financing system. They were able to get several banking reform provisions into the Road to Housing bill. They've also floated new financial reforms to change and rescind banking rules codified in the Dodd-Frank Act and the Basel III accords after the subprime mortgage crisis. Republicans say they've chased smaller banks out of the housing market.

This summer, Hill called Dodd-Frank a "massive overreaction."

"For our smaller banks, the new regulations were a poor fit, burying them in compliance work and bureaucratic red tape," Hill said. "Instead of helping customers, their attention was diverted to filling out forms and satisfying examiners."

Mortgage reform is also a lever Congress can pull. A bipartisan group of six members of Congress pitched the First-Time Homebuyer Affordability Act this summer. It would expand access to low-interest mortgages for first-time homebuyers.

"By expanding access to financing, we can help more hardworking families achieve homeownership, strengthen our communities, and build long-term financial security," said Rep. Darin LaHood (R-IL) said.

Then there's the matter of new construction. Democrats and Republicans found some bipartisan ground in cutting some of the federal red tape that blocks construction of new homes.

Republicans also said they favor rolling back Build America Buy America provisions. Builders, they say, have complained that the sourcing requirements are time-consuming and expensive. But they've also targeted some environmental regulations that Democrats passed.

Dennis Shea, chair of the J. Ronald Terwilliger Center for Housing Policy at the Bipartisan Policy Center, tells Realtor.com that some public housing authorities saw costs climb by double digits. The U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development's waiver process is every bit as time-consuming for builders.

"A sentiment exists in both parties to change the rules, but ultimately it takes an act of Congress to exempt housing," Shea said.

Hill and other lawmakers such as Rep. Emanuel Cleaver (D-MO) have said that Congress' action should shift some burden onto states to implement reforms at the local and state level. The American Enterprise Institute said this year that state and local policy changes could boost annual homebuilding by six figures. But many of those new homes are accessory dwelling units.

Congress and the White Hose also found common ground on manufactured housing reform. All sides recognize they need to keep eyeing supply-side reforms, says Cassidy.

"Ultimately, the problem in this country is supply," Cassidy adds. "You could lower rates to 2% tomorrow, and all that will do is increase home values. It's not going to solve the underlying issue."

The other big question will be funding of the many provisions created under the Road to Housing Act. While the provisions in the bill were bipartisan, Congress must still approve funding for some of those programs. Some of those discussions are coming in the 2028 fiscal budget and beyond.

Over the months of negotiations on the Road to Housing bill, some Republicans took issue with unfunded mandates in it. Concerns about funding programs, and at what level, could spill over into congressional proceedings in coming years.

Tony Marquez, director of American University's real estate program, who has a long career in real estate and banking, tells Realtor.com that the industry is still amenable to a few different ideas for how to solve housing. It doesn't rely on one side's control of the government.

"My un-professional assessment is we'll have a certain amount of gridlock over a number of years where Democrats may come up with some suggestions," Marquez says. "But the answer to affordable housing is not just one silver bullet. It can have many elements."

Tristan Navera is a senior reporter on housing policy, covering trends and solutions in the housing market from Washington, DC. He was previously a senior reporter at Bloomberg Law, and before that covered real estate for the Washington Business Journal. Earlier in his career, he spent a decade reporting on business and real estate in Dayton and Columbus, OH. A Cincinnati native, he holds a journalism degree from Ohio University.
